It’s hard to say out loud because it makes me feel awful, especially being pregnant, but… I’ve had thoughts like I wish I could just not wake up. Not like I’ve made a plan or anything — I haven’t — but more like this feeling of wanting everything to stop for a while, or wanting to disappear so I don’t have to feel like this.\n\nThat’s actually what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment. I kept trying to tell myself I was just overwhelmed, but when those thoughts started coming up, even passively, I thought… okay, this isn’t something I should just keep to myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have noticed that. It’s like everything I do is slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even getting myself from one task to the next feels harder and takes longer than it should.\n\nAt the clinic, it’s been really obvious to me. Things that used to be automatic — like gathering wound care supplies, setting things up, finishing my charting — I still do them, but I feel like I’m dragging through each step. I’ll catch myself just standing there for a second, trying to remember what I was about to grab, or moving so slowly that I’m aware it’s taking me longer than it should.\n\nAnd speaking too, I think. Sometimes it takes me longer to answer people, like there’s this delay between hearing them and getting the words out. It makes me feel embarrassed because I’m used to being more efficient, especially at work. Right now I just feel… slow. Everything feels heavy and delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been weighing myself consistently, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost a certain amount when I don’t really know.\n\nBut my appetite has definitely changed. I know I should be eating regularly, especially being pregnant, and I feel guilty about it, but most days food just sounds… unappealing. Like I’ll think, okay, I need to eat something real, and then nothing sounds good, or the idea of making something feels like too much.\n\nThere have been days at the clinic where I’ll get halfway through my shift and suddenly realize I’ve only had crackers, or coffee, or just something small like that all day. Not because I’m trying to restrict or anything — it’s more like I don’t feel interested in food until I realize I’m shaky or just know I should probably eat. It makes me feel bad, because I know it’s not great, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… it’s more the opposite for me. I don’t feel like I’m crawling out of my skin or needing to pace. I feel slowed down.\n\nLike, everything I do feels heavy and delayed, almost like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic that I normally wouldn’t think twice about — gathering wound care supplies, setting things up, finishing my charting — they’ve been taking me noticeably longer than they should. I’ll be doing something routine and it’s like my body and my brain are both lagging behind.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m having trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s hard to get myself moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your pregnancy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I feel guilty about the pregnancy itself. I mean… I don’t think of the baby as something I resent, exactly, or like I’m blaming the pregnancy.\n\nBut I do feel guilty around it, if that makes sense. Like guilty that I’m not feeling the way I think I’m supposed to feel, or that I’m not handling things better. I keep thinking I should be more… grateful, or excited, or steady. And when I’m not, I start wondering what that says about me.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it guilt about being pregnant. It’s more guilt about me — about not feeling like I’m doing this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having an inability to sit still recently?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no. I wouldn’t describe it as not being able to sit still. It’s actually more like the opposite has been happening.\n\nEverything feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things I do all the time at the clinic — just routine stuff, like gathering wound care supplies or getting myself organized to finish charting — it takes me noticeably longer than it should. And I’m aware of it while it’s happening, which makes it frustrating, because I know how quickly I normally can do those things.\n\nSo it’s not that I’m bouncing my leg or pacing or feeling like I have to move. It’s more like I have to push myself through every step, and my body just isn’t keeping up the way it usually does.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d use the word worthless. That feels… really harsh, and I’m not sure I sit there thinking, “I’m worthless,” exactly.\n\nI do get down on myself, though. Like I’ll compare how I’m doing to how I think I should be doing, and I’ll feel disappointed in myself or like I’m not measuring up. But “worthless” sounds more absolute than what I usually think. It’s more like… I feel inadequate sometimes, or like I’m falling short, not that I have no value at all.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not exactly worthless — but I’m definitely not feeling very good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty in concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes… definitely. That’s actually been one of the more noticeable things for me, because I usually rely on being pretty sharp at work.\n\nLately my brain feels foggy, like I can’t hold things in my head the same way. I’ll read a medication order, and then I’ll have to read it again, and sometimes again, because it just doesn’t feel like it stuck. Same with wound-care notes — I’ll be looking at something that should be straightforward, something I’ve done so many times, and I’ll catch myself rereading the instructions because I don’t trust that I absorbed it correctly the first time.\n\nAnd decisions are harder too, even small ones that I’d normally make almost automatically. I’ll freeze for a second and start second-guessing myself, like, “Is this the right supply? Did I miss something? Should I check the note again?” It’s not that I don’t know how to do my job, exactly. It’s more like there’s this delay between knowing and being able to act on it, and then I get stuck questioning myself.\n\nSo yes, concentrating and making decisions have both been harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of hopelessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think hopelessness is probably part of it, though it’s hard for me to say that word out loud.\n\nIt’s more like, for the past few weeks, I’ve had this heavy sadness sitting on me almost all day. Like a pressure, almost. I’ll still go to work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I can smile at patients or act normal enough when I need to, but inside it feels like I’m barely keeping myself together. It’s exhausting pretending I’m okay when I’m really not.\n\nI don’t know if I’m constantly thinking, “nothing will ever get better,” exactly, but it does feel like I can’t see past this. Like I’m just trying to get through the next hour or the next shift, and everything feels heavier than it should. So… yes, I guess I have been feeling pretty hopeless, or at least like it’s hard to imagine feeling like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth?\n\nPatient: Not really pacing, no. I wouldn’t say I feel like I have to keep moving or that I’m restless in that way.\n\nIf anything, it’s almost the opposite. Everything I do feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things at the clinic that should be quick and automatic take me noticeably longer than they should. Like gathering wound supplies — normally I can just grab what I need without thinking much about it, but lately I feel like I’m moving in slow motion, checking and rechecking, taking forever to get everything together.\n\nAnd charting too… I’ll sit down to finish notes that shouldn’t take that long, and it’s like I can’t make myself move through them at a normal pace. I’m doing it, but it feels dragged out, like my body and my brain are both lagging. So no, not pacing back and forth — more like everything has gotten slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan. I need to be really clear about that because even saying this out loud makes me feel scared and ashamed.\n\nWhat has been happening is more like… I’ll have thoughts like, “I wish I just wouldn’t wake up,” or “I don’t want to keep feeling like this.” Not that I’ve sat down and figured out how to hurt myself, or that I have some plan I’m intending to follow. I don’t. But the thoughts were upsetting enough that I finally said something at my prenatal appointment, because it scared me that my mind was even going there.\n\nIt feels awful to admit, especially because I know how serious that sounds, but no — I don’t have a plan to harm myself. I’ve just had those thoughts about not wanting to wake up, and that’s what frightened me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your depressive symptoms have been significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… this feels pretty bad to me right now, and it’s hard to compare it to anything when I’m in the middle of it.\n\nI don’t think I can point to a clear time where I’d say, “Oh, that was definitely worse than this.” There have been hard periods before, like stressful stretches where I felt overwhelmed or down, but I don’t know if I’d describe them as significantly worse. It’s more that right now feels heavy and ongoing enough that it’s hard for me to judge it against the past.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. Or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt nearly normal, without these depressive symptoms, over the past three weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Over the past three weeks, I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I felt like myself or close to normal.\n\nIt’s been kind of continuous since shortly after I found out I was pregnant. I remember thinking at first maybe it was just the shock or the adjustment, like maybe once it sank in I’d feel more steady, but it hasn’t really lifted in that way. There might be little moments where I’m distracted or I can get through something I need to do, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a period where the depressive feelings were gone or I felt nearly normal.\n\nSo… no, not in the last three weeks. It’s pretty much been there the whole time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really an urge to move around, no. It’s actually kind of the opposite for me.

Everything feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even at the clinic, things that should be automatic for me take longer than they should. Like gathering wound supplies — normally I know exactly what I need and I can just do it, but lately I feel like I’m moving so slowly, double-checking myself, taking forever. And finishing charting feels like it drags on and on, even when it’s not complicated.

It’s frustrating because I can tell I’m not moving at my normal pace. It’s not that I’m restless or can’t sit still — it’s more like my body and my brain are both delayed, and simple tasks take so much more effort than they used to.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because… honestly, for the past few weeks I’ve just felt really weighed down. Like there’s this heavy, sad pressure sitting on me almost all day. It’s like I’m present on the outside, but inside I’m just… really sad, almost all the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about three weeks now, I think. At first I thought maybe I was just overwhelmed or reacting to the news, but it hasn’t really lifted. It’s been most days, for those three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually been one of the things that made me think something is really off, because I’m usually someone who needs my little routines after work to feel like myself again.\n\nAfter a shift, especially if it was stressful, I used to come home and paint for a while, or I’d go to yoga, or sometimes salsa class if I had the energy. Those were things I genuinely looked forward to, like… they helped me decompress and get out of my head. I mean… I’ve tried a couple times to force myself, because I keep thinking maybe if I just start painting, or if I just get myself to yoga, something will click and I’ll feel a little more normal. But it doesn’t really happen.\n\nAfter my shifts, I used to actually look forward to coming home and painting, or going to yoga, or salsa if I had the energy. It felt like it helped me shake off the day. Now it’s like I get home and I just sit on the couch, and even looking at my paints feels like another task. Texts come in and I see them, but I ignore them because replying feels like too much effort. It’s not even that I’m enjoying being alone or relaxing… it just feels like none of it is worth starting.\n\nSo even when I do try, the pleasure isn’t really there. It feels flat, like I’m just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to say out loud because it makes me feel awful, especially being pregnant, but… I’ve had thoughts like I wish I could just not wake up. Not like I’ve made a plan or anything — I haven’t — but more like this feeling of wanting everything to stop for a while, or wanting to disappear so I don’t have to feel like this.\n\nThat’s actually what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment. I kept trying to tell myself I was just overwhelmed, but when those thoughts started coming up, even passively, I thought… okay, this isn’t something I should just keep to myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have noticed that. It’s like everything I do is slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even getting myself from one task to the next feels harder and takes longer than it should.\n\nAt the clinic, it’s been really obvious to me. Things that used to be automatic — like gathering wound care supplies, setting things up, finishing my charting — I still do them, but I feel like I’m dragging through each step. I’ll catch myself just standing there for a second, trying to remember what I was about to grab, or moving so slowly that I’m aware it’s taking me longer than it should.\n\nAnd speaking too, I think. Sometimes it takes me longer to answer people, like there’s this delay between hearing them and getting the words out. It makes me feel embarrassed because I’m used to being more efficient, especially at work. Right now I just feel… slow. Everything feels heavy and delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been weighing myself consistently, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost a certain amount when I don’t really know.\n\nBut my appetite has definitely changed. I know I should be eating regularly, especially being pregnant, and I feel guilty about it, but most days food just sounds… unappealing. Like I’ll think, okay, I need to eat something real, and then nothing sounds good, or the idea of making something feels like too much.\n\nThere have been days at the clinic where I’ll get halfway through my shift and suddenly realize I’ve only had crackers, or coffee, or just something small like that all day. Not because I’m trying to restrict or anything — it’s more like I don’t feel interested in food until I realize I’m shaky or just know I should probably eat. It makes me feel bad, because I know it’s not great, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… it’s more the opposite for me. I don’t feel like I’m crawling out of my skin or needing to pace. I feel slowed down.\n\nLike, everything I do feels heavy and delayed, almost like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic that I normally wouldn’t think twice about — gathering wound care supplies, setting things up, finishing my charting — they’ve been taking me noticeably longer than they should. I’ll be doing something routine and it’s like my body and my brain are both lagging behind.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m having trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s hard to get myself moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your pregnancy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I feel guilty about the pregnancy itself. I mean… I don’t think of the baby as something I resent, exactly, or like I’m blaming the pregnancy.\n\nBut I do feel guilty around it, if that makes sense. Like guilty that I’m not feeling the way I think I’m supposed to feel, or that I’m not handling things better. I keep thinking I should be more… grateful, or excited, or steady. And when I’m not, I start wondering what that says about me.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it guilt about being pregnant. It’s more guilt about me — about not feeling like I’m doing this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having an inability to sit still recently?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no. I wouldn’t describe it as not being able to sit still. It’s actually more like the opposite has been happening.\n\nEverything feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things I do all the time at the clinic — just routine stuff, like gathering wound care supplies or getting myself organized to finish charting — it takes me noticeably longer than it should. And I’m aware of it while it’s happening, which makes it frustrating, because I know how quickly I normally can do those things.\n\nSo it’s not that I’m bouncing my leg or pacing or feeling like I have to move. It’s more like I have to push myself through every step, and my body just isn’t keeping up the way it usually does.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d use the word worthless. That feels… really harsh, and I’m not sure I sit there thinking, “I’m worthless,” exactly.\n\nI do get down on myself, though. Like I’ll compare how I’m doing to how I think I should be doing, and I’ll feel disappointed in myself or like I’m not measuring up. But “worthless” sounds more absolute than what I usually think. It’s more like… I feel inadequate sometimes, or like I’m falling short, not that I have no value at all.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not exactly worthless — but I’m definitely not feeling very good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty in concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes… definitely. That’s actually been one of the more noticeable things for me, because I usually rely on being pretty sharp at work.\n\nLately my brain feels foggy, like I can’t hold things in my head the same way. I’ll read a medication order, and then I’ll have to read it again, and sometimes again, because it just doesn’t feel like it stuck. Same with wound-care notes — I’ll be looking at something that should be straightforward, something I’ve done so many times, and I’ll catch myself rereading the instructions because I don’t trust that I absorbed it correctly the first time.\n\nAnd decisions are harder too, even small ones that I’d normally make almost automatically. I’ll freeze for a second and start second-guessing myself, like, “Is this the right supply? Did I miss something? Should I check the note again?” It’s not that I don’t know how to do my job, exactly. It’s more like there’s this delay between knowing and being able to act on it, and then I get stuck questioning myself.\n\nSo yes, concentrating and making decisions have both been harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of hopelessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think hopelessness is probably part of it, though it’s hard for me to say that word out loud.\n\nIt’s more like, for the past few weeks, I’ve had this heavy sadness sitting on me almost all day. Like a pressure, almost. I’ll still go to work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I can smile at patients or act normal enough when I need to, but inside it feels like I’m barely keeping myself together. It’s exhausting pretending I’m okay when I’m really not.\n\nI don’t know if I’m constantly thinking, “nothing will ever get better,” exactly, but it does feel like I can’t see past this. Like I’m just trying to get through the next hour or the next shift, and everything feels heavier than it should. Like gathering wound supplies — normally I can just grab what I need without thinking much about it, but lately I feel like I’m moving in slow motion, checking and rechecking, taking forever to get everything together.\n\nAnd charting too… I’ll sit down to finish notes that shouldn’t take that long, and it’s like I can’t make myself move through them at a normal pace. I’m doing it, but it feels dragged out, like my body and my brain are both lagging. So no, not pacing back and forth — more like everything has gotten slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan. Everything feels heavy and delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been weighing myself consistently, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost a certain amount when I don’t really know.\n\nBut my appetite has definitely changed. I know I should be eating regularly, especially being pregnant, and I feel guilty about it, but most days food just sounds… unappealing. Like I’ll think, okay, I need to eat something real, and then nothing sounds good, or the idea of making something feels like too much.\n\nThere have been days at the clinic where I’ll get halfway through my shift and suddenly realize I’ve only had crackers, or coffee, or just something small like that all day. Not because I’m trying to restrict or anything — it’s more like I don’t feel interested in food until I realize I’m shaky or just know I should probably eat. It makes me feel bad, because I know it’s not great, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… it’s more the opposite for me. I don’t feel like I’m crawling out of my skin or needing to pace. I feel slowed down.\n\nLike, everything I do feels heavy and delayed, almost like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic that I normally wouldn’t think twice about — gathering wound care supplies, setting things up, finishing my charting — they’ve been taking me noticeably longer than they should. I’ll be doing something routine and it’s like my body and my brain are both lagging behind.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m having trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s hard to get myself moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your pregnancy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I feel guilty about the pregnancy itself. I mean… I don’t think of the baby as something I resent, exactly, or like I’m blaming the pregnancy.\n\nBut I do feel guilty around it, if that makes sense. Like guilty that I’m not feeling the way I think I’m supposed to feel, or that I’m not handling things better. I keep thinking I should be more… grateful, or excited, or steady. And when I’m not, I start wondering what that says about me.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it guilt about being pregnant. It’s more guilt about me — about not feeling like I’m doing this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having an inability to sit still recently?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no. I wouldn’t describe it as not being able to sit still. It’s actually more like the opposite has been happening.\n\nEverything feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things I do all the time at the clinic — just routine stuff, like gathering wound care supplies or getting myself organized to finish charting — it takes me noticeably longer than it should. And I’m aware of it while it’s happening, which makes it frustrating, because I know how quickly I normally can do those things.\n\nSo it’s not that I’m bouncing my leg or pacing or feeling like I have to move. It’s more like I have to push myself through every step, and my body just isn’t keeping up the way it usually does.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d use the word worthless. That feels… really harsh, and I’m not sure I sit there thinking, “I’m worthless,” exactly.\n\nI do get down on myself, though. Like I’ll compare how I’m doing to how I think I should be doing, and I’ll feel disappointed in myself or like I’m not measuring up. But “worthless” sounds more absolute than what I usually think. It’s more like… I feel inadequate sometimes, or like I’m falling short, not that I have no value at all.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not exactly worthless — but I’m definitely not feeling very good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty in concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes… definitely. That’s actually been one of the more noticeable things for me, because I usually rely on being pretty sharp at work.\n\nLately my brain feels foggy, like I can’t hold things in my head the same way. I’ll read a medication order, and then I’ll have to read it again, and sometimes again, because it just doesn’t feel like it stuck. Same with wound-care notes — I’ll be looking at something that should be straightforward, something I’ve done so many times, and I’ll catch myself rereading the instructions because I don’t trust that I absorbed it correctly the first time.\n\nAnd decisions are harder too, even small ones that I’d normally make almost automatically. I’ll freeze for a second and start second-guessing myself, like, “Is this the right supply? Did I miss something? Should I check the note again?” It’s not that I don’t know how to do my job, exactly. It’s more like there’s this delay between knowing and being able to act on it, and then I get stuck questioning myself.\n\nSo yes, concentrating and making decisions have both been harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of hopelessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think hopelessness is probably part of it, though it’s hard for me to say that word out loud.\n\nIt’s more like, for the past few weeks, I’ve had this heavy sadness sitting on me almost all day. Like a pressure, almost. I’ll still go to work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I can smile at patients or act normal enough when I need to, but inside it feels like I’m barely keeping myself together. It’s exhausting pretending I’m okay when I’m really not.\n\nI don’t know if I’m constantly thinking, “nothing will ever get better,” exactly, but it does feel like I can’t see past this. Like I’m just trying to get through the next hour or the next shift, and everything feels heavier than it should. So… yes, I guess I have been feeling pretty hopeless, or at least like it’s hard to imagine feeling like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth?\n\nPatient: Not really pacing, no. I wouldn’t say I feel like I have to keep moving or that I’m restless in that way.\n\nIf anything, it’s almost the opposite. Everything I do feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things at the clinic that should be quick and automatic take me noticeably longer than they should. Like gathering wound supplies — normally I can just grab what I need without thinking much about it, but lately I feel like I’m moving in slow motion, checking and rechecking, taking forever to get everything together.\n\nAnd charting too… I’ll sit down to finish notes that shouldn’t take that long, and it’s like I can’t make myself move through them at a normal pace. I’m doing it, but it feels dragged out, like my body and my brain are both lagging. So no, not pacing back and forth — more like everything has gotten slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan. I need to be really clear about that because even saying this out loud makes me feel scared and ashamed.\n\nWhat has been happening is more like… I’ll have thoughts like, “I wish I just wouldn’t wake up,” or “I don’t want to keep feeling like this.” Not that I’ve sat down and figured out how to hurt myself, or that I have some plan I’m intending to follow. I don’t. I’ve just had those thoughts about not wanting to wake up, and that’s what frightened me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your depressive symptoms have been significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… this feels pretty bad to me right now, and it’s hard to compare it to anything when I’m in the middle of it.\n\nI don’t think I can point to a clear time where I’d say, “Oh, that was definitely worse than this.” There have been hard periods before, like stressful stretches where I felt overwhelmed or down, but I don’t know if I’d describe them as significantly worse. It’s more that right now feels heavy and ongoing enough that it’s hard for me to judge it against the past.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. Or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt nearly normal, without these depressive symptoms, over the past three weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Over the past three weeks, I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I felt like myself or close to normal.\n\nIt’s been kind of continuous since shortly after I found out I was pregnant. Yeah… some. I mean, I’m still functioning on the outside, so it’s not like I’m not showering at all or I’m not showing up anywhere. I am still making it to work, and I’m getting dressed and doing the basic things because I sort of have to.

Like I’ll think, okay, I need to eat something real, and then nothing sounds good, or the idea of making something feels like too much.\n\nThere have been days at the clinic where I’ll get halfway through my shift and suddenly realize I’ve only had crackers, or coffee, or just something small like that all day. Not because I’m trying to restrict or anything — it’s more like I don’t feel interested in food until I realize I’m shaky or just know I should probably eat. It makes me feel bad, because I know it’s not great, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… it’s more the opposite for me. I don’t feel like I’m crawling out of my skin or needing to pace. I feel slowed down.\n\nLike, everything I do feels heavy and delayed, almost like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic that I normally wouldn’t think twice about — gathering wound care supplies, setting things up, finishing my charting — they’ve been taking me noticeably longer than they should. I’ll be doing something routine and it’s like my body and my brain are both lagging behind.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m having trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s hard to get myself moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your pregnancy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I feel guilty about the pregnancy itself. I mean… I don’t think of the baby as something I resent, exactly, or like I’m blaming the pregnancy.\n\nBut I do feel guilty around it, if that makes sense. Like guilty that I’m not feeling the way I think I’m supposed to feel, or that I’m not handling things better. I keep thinking I should be more… grateful, or excited, or steady. And when I’m not, I start wondering what that says about me.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it guilt about being pregnant. It’s more guilt about me — about not feeling like I’m doing this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having an inability to sit still recently?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no. I wouldn’t describe it as not being able to sit still. It’s actually more like the opposite has been happening.\n\nEverything feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things I do all the time at the clinic — just routine stuff, like gathering wound care supplies or getting myself organized to finish charting — it takes me noticeably longer than it should. And I’m aware of it while it’s happening, which makes it frustrating, because I know how quickly I normally can do those things.\n\nSo it’s not that I’m bouncing my leg or pacing or feeling like I have to move. It’s more like I have to push myself through every step, and my body just isn’t keeping up the way it usually does.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d use the word worthless. That feels… really harsh, and I’m not sure I sit there thinking, “I’m worthless,” exactly.\n\nI do get down on myself, though. Like I’ll compare how I’m doing to how I think I should be doing, and I’ll feel disappointed in myself or like I’m not measuring up. But “worthless” sounds more absolute than what I usually think. It’s more like… I feel inadequate sometimes, or like I’m falling short, not that I have no value at all.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not exactly worthless — but I’m definitely not feeling very good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty in concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes… definitely. That’s actually been one of the more noticeable things for me, because I usually rely on being pretty sharp at work.\n\nLately my brain feels foggy, like I can’t hold things in my head the same way. I’ll read a medication order, and then I’ll have to read it again, and sometimes again, because it just doesn’t feel like it stuck. Same with wound-care notes — I’ll be looking at something that should be straightforward, something I’ve done so many times, and I’ll catch myself rereading the instructions because I don’t trust that I absorbed it correctly the first time.\n\nAnd decisions are harder too, even small ones that I’d normally make almost automatically. I’ll freeze for a second and start second-guessing myself, like, “Is this the right supply? Did I miss something? Should I check the note again?” It’s not that I don’t know how to do my job, exactly. It’s more like there’s this delay between knowing and being able to act on it, and then I get stuck questioning myself.\n\nSo yes, concentrating and making decisions have both been harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of hopelessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think hopelessness is probably part of it, though it’s hard for me to say that word out loud.\n\nIt’s more like, for the past few weeks, I’ve had this heavy sadness sitting on me almost all day. Like a pressure, almost. I’ll still go to work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I can smile at patients or act normal enough when I need to, but inside it feels like I’m barely keeping myself together. It’s exhausting pretending I’m okay when I’m really not.\n\nI don’t know if I’m constantly thinking, “nothing will ever get better,” exactly, but it does feel like I can’t see past this. Like I’m just trying to get through the next hour or the next shift, and everything feels heavier than it should. So… yes, I guess I have been feeling pretty hopeless, or at least like it’s hard to imagine feeling like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth?\n\nPatient: Not really pacing, no. I wouldn’t say I feel like I have to keep moving or that I’m restless in that way.\n\nIf anything, it’s almost the opposite. Everything I do feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even things at the clinic that should be quick and automatic take me noticeably longer than they should. Like gathering wound supplies — normally I can just grab what I need without thinking much about it, but lately I feel like I’m moving in slow motion, checking and rechecking, taking forever to get everything together.\n\nAnd charting too… I’ll sit down to finish notes that shouldn’t take that long, and it’s like I can’t make myself move through them at a normal pace. I’m doing it, but it feels dragged out, like my body and my brain are both lagging. So no, not pacing back and forth — more like everything has gotten slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t made a plan. I need to be really clear about that because even saying this out loud makes me feel scared and ashamed.\n\nWhat has been happening is more like… I’ll have thoughts like, “I wish I just wouldn’t wake up,” or “I don’t want to keep feeling like this.” Not that I’ve sat down and figured out how to hurt myself, or that I have some plan I’m intending to follow. I don’t. But the thoughts were upsetting enough that I finally said something at my prenatal appointment, because it scared me that my mind was even going there.\n\nIt feels awful to admit, especially because I know how serious that sounds, but no — I don’t have a plan to harm myself. I’ve just had those thoughts about not wanting to wake up, and that’s what frightened me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your depressive symptoms have been significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… this feels pretty bad to me right now, and it’s hard to compare it to anything when I’m in the middle of it.\n\nI don’t think I can point to a clear time where I’d say, “Oh, that was definitely worse than this.” There have been hard periods before, like stressful stretches where I felt overwhelmed or down, but I don’t know if I’d describe them as significantly worse. It’s more that right now feels heavy and ongoing enough that it’s hard for me to judge it against the past.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. Or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt nearly normal, without these depressive symptoms, over the past three weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Over the past three weeks, I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I felt like myself or close to normal.\n\nIt’s been kind of continuous since shortly after I found out I was pregnant. I remember thinking at first maybe it was just the shock or the adjustment, like maybe once it sank in I’d feel more steady, but it hasn’t really lifted in that way. There might be little moments where I’m distracted or I can get through something I need to do, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a period where the depressive feelings were gone or I felt nearly normal.\n\nSo… no, not in the last three weeks. It’s pretty much been there the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an inability to sit still or a strong urge to move around?\n\nPatient: Not really an urge to move around, no. It’s actually kind of the opposite for me.\n\nEverything feels slowed down, like I’m moving through syrup or something. Even at the clinic, things that should be automatic for me take longer than they should. Like gathering wound supplies — normally I know exactly what I need and I can just do it, but lately I feel like I’m moving so slowly, double-checking myself, taking forever. And finishing charting feels like it drags on and on, even when it’s not complicated.\n\nIt’s frustrating because I can tell I’m not moving at my normal pace. It’s not that I’m restless or can’t sit still — it’s more like my body and my brain are both delayed, and simple tasks take so much more effort than they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulty in performing daily activities such as showering, dressing, or managing your household due to your current emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I mean, I’m still functioning on the outside, so it’s not like I’m not showering at all or I’m not showing up anywhere. I am still making it to work, and I’m getting dressed and doing the basic things because I sort of have to.\n\nBut it’s definitely affecting things. At work, my supervisor has already noticed that my charting isn’t always complete, which is really embarrassing for me because that’s not normally how I am. I keep thinking I’ll go back and finish something or catch up, and then it just feels like everything piles up or I can’t keep it all straight the way I usually can.\n\nAt home, I wouldn’t say everything has completely fallen apart, but I’m not on top of things the way I usually am. And socially… I’ve been pulling away a lot. I haven’t really been reaching out to friends, and I’ve been avoiding the things that normally help me feel more grounded or steady. It’s like I know those things probably matter, but I don’t have it in me to keep up with them right now.\n\nSo, yes, it’s affecting my daily life. I’m still pushing through, but it’s taking a lot more effort, and people are starting to notice.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt great, but I don’t know if I can point to a clear, major change like “I sleep exactly this many hours now” or anything like that.
